Reliability per golden

One line is one golden on one target in one mode (one perturbation in P), over its repeats. pass^k is the chance that k repeats all pass, pass@k that one of k passes; their interval is the Wilson interval of the per-run rate raised the same way. Unreliability is the 90th minus the 10th percentile of the share of passing blocks across repeats.

The teacher-forced pass rate minus the free-running pass rate over the blocks both modes ran. Positive: each block is right on its own and the agent loses it across the succession. Interval: blocks resampled within a golden, goldens across the campaign.

Recovery after a deviation

For every perturbed run whose perturbation sets a recovery: immediate when the agent passes at its stage within the blocks allowed, late after, never when it does not come back. Collateral counts the blocks after the injection that fail what passed in the free-running reference. Safe action: no forbidden or unnamed consequential act during the detour. Clarified: a question before the first consequential act.

Spread

Mode F, no generated cell in these specs: one golden is one cell. A cell rate takes its conversations as clusters when it holds two or more, else the Wilson interval over its runs. A cell is undecided when its interval straddles its reference minus the margin (15.0 points); the reference is the baseline cell rate when a baseline is given, else 100%.

How the numbers are made

  • Every interval is two-sided at 95%.
  • Wilson: the score interval on k of n, used for a rate over independent units (the repeats of one golden, the goldens of a campaign).
  • Cluster bootstrap: 2000 percentile resamples of whole clusters, seed 1, so the same store gives the same page. Anything pooled over blocks takes one run (one conversation) as one cluster; anything pooled over the runs of a mode takes one golden as one cluster.
  • Wilson over clusters: a pass rate whose values all agree (every block passes, or none does) leaves a bootstrap no width, so it takes the Wilson interval with each cluster counted once.
  • Newcombe: the paired difference of two rates, for baseline against candidate.
  • No interval: a bootstrap over a single cluster has none; the page says so instead of drawing one.
  • A run passes when it completes, every block passes and the end state matches.
